L2149: ## 21. Labeling Summary of Considerations and Key Additional Information
L2150: ## Prescribing Information
L2151: ## General
L2152: This Prescribing Information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the major changes made to the single PI submitted by the Applicant on September 3, 2021, for VOQUEZNA™ TRIPLE PAK and VOQUEZNA™ DUAL PAK. The following general revisions were made throughout the PI:
L2153: - The TRADENAME was changed from [REDACTED](b) (4) TRIPLE PAK and [REDACTED](b) (4) DUAL PAK to VOQUEZNA™ TRIPLE PAK and VOQUEZNA™ DUAL PAK throughout the PI based on the final recommendations of the Division of Medication Error Prevention and Analysis 1 (DMEPA 1). Refer to the DMEPA 1 review dated April 26, 2022, for additional details.
- Extensive revisions (e.g., reformatting and streamlining of the information) were made to the single PI submitted by the Applicant to ensure that the PI clearly identifies which risks belong to which product. Specifically, the important safety/drug interaction information pertaining to VOQUEZNA™ TRIPLE PAK and VOQUEZNA™ DUAL PAK vs. important safety/drug interaction information pertaining only to VOQUEZNA™ TRIPLE PAK due to the clarithromycin component were reformatted with specific headings, subheadings and streamlining of the information as appropriate.
L2154: [REDACTED](b) (4) was removed from several sections of the PI because it is not generally available in the U.S.
L2155: - *Clostridium difficile* was changed to the new nomenclature *Clostridioides difficile* throughout PI.
L2156: ## HIGHLIGHTS OF PRESCRIBING INFORMATION (HL) AND TABLE OF CONTENTS (TOC)
L2157: The HL and the TOC sections of the PI were revised for consistency with the changes made to the rest of the PI.
L2158: ## FULL PRESCRIBING INFORMATION
L2159: ## 2. DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION
L2160: ## 2.3 Missed Doses
L2161: The specific time of 4 hours within which VOQUEZNA TRIPLE PAK and VOQUEZNA DUAL PAK should be administered after a missed dose was added. The Applicant provided supportive PK simulations showing that missing a dose by up to 4 hours will have minimal impact on the exposure of vonoprazan prior to and after the missed dose.
L2162: NDA 215152: Voquezna Triple Pak (vonoprazan/clarithromycin/amoxicillin)
L2163: NDA 215153: Voquezna Dual Pak (vonoprazan/amoxicillin)
L2164: ## 5. WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS
L2165: ## 5.1 Warnings and Precautions for VOQUEZNA TRIPLE PAK and VOQUEZNA DUAL PAK
L2166: ## Severe Cutaneous Adverse Reactions
L2167: Severe cutaneous adverse reactions were removed from the hypersensitivity warning and precaution (W&amp;P) subheading and provided under a separate W&amp;P subheading, to be consistent with the PI for other *H. pylori* combination products (e.g., Talicia and Omeclamox-Pak). Similar revisions were made to the Adverse Reactions section.
L2168: ## Rash with Use in Patients with Mononucleosis
L2169: The risk mitigation statement for this warning and precaution was changed from (b) (4) to “Avoid use of VOQUEZNA TRIPLE PAK and VOQUEZNA DUAL PAK in patients with mononucleosis.” This revision was per the labeling recommendations in the guidance for industry: *Warnings and Precautions, Contraindications, and Boxed Warning Sections of Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products – Content and Format* (October 2011), that terminology that generally infers a contraindication (e.g., “Do not use” or “Drug X should not be used”) should not appear in the WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS section.
L2170: ## 5.2. Additional Warnings and Precautions for TRADENAME TRIPLE PAK Due to the Clarithromycin Component
L2171: ## Serious Adverse Reactions Due to Concomitant Use of Clarithromycin with Other Drugs
L2172: **Benzodiazepines:** A new risk mitigation statement was added to this warning and precaution as follows: “Closely monitor patients for signs or symptoms of increased or prolonged central nervous system effects when benzodiazepines such as triazolam or midazolam are used concomitantly with VOQUEZNA TRIPLE PAK [see Drug Interactions (7.2)].” This revision was per the labeling recommendations in the guidance for industry: *Warnings and Precautions, Contraindications, and Boxed Warning Sections of Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products – Content and Format* (October 2011), to include information on the steps to take to decrease the likelihood, shorten the duration, or minimize the severity of an adverse reaction in the description of the W&amp;P, if known and important to clinical decision making.

L2200: ## 22. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L2201: The following Postmarketing Requirements (PMRs) were agreed to by the Applicant on April 26, 2022.
L2202: ## PMR 4270-1
L2203: Conduct a prospective, registry based observational exposure cohort study that compares the maternal, fetal, and infant outcomes of women exposed to vonoprazan-containing products during pregnancy to an unexposed control population. The registry should be designed to detect and record major and minor congenital malformations, spontaneous abortions, stillbirths, elective terminations, small for gestational age, preterm births, and any other adverse pregnancy outcomes. These outcomes will be assessed throughout pregnancy. Infant outcomes, including effects on postnatal growth and development, will be assessed through at least the first year of life.
L2204: ## PMR Schedule Milestones
L2205: - Draft Protocol Submission: November 2022
- Final Protocol Submission: July 2023
- Interim Report Submission: July 2024, July 2025, July 2026, July 2027, July 2028, July 2029, July 2030, July 2031, July 2032, July 2033, July 2034, July 2035
- Study Completion: July 2035
L2206: NDA 215152: Voquezna Triple Pak (vonoprazan/clarithromycin/amoxicillin)
L2207: NDA 215153: Voquezna Dual Pak (vonoprazan/amoxicillin)
L2208: - Final report Submission: April 2036
L2209: ## PMR 4270-2
L2210: An additional pregnancy study that uses a different design from the Pregnancy Registry (for example, a retrospective cohort study using claims or electronic medical record data or a case control study) to assess major congenital malformations, spontaneous abortions, stillbirths, and small for gestational age and preterm birth in women exposed to vonoprazan-containing products during pregnancy compared to an unexposed control population.
L2211: ## PMR Schedule Milestones

- Final Protocol Submission: July 2023
- Interim Report Submission: July 2024, July 2025, July 2026, July 2027, July 2028, July 2029, July 2030
- Study Completion: July 2030
- Final Report Submission: April 2031
L2213: ## PMR 4270-3
L2214: Conduct a lactation study (milk only) in lactating women who have received vonoprazan-containing products to assess concentrations of vonoprazan in breast milk using a validated assay. A mother-infant pair study may be required in the future depending on the results of this milk-only study.
L2215: ## PMR Schedule Milestones
L2216: - Draft Protocol Submission: November 2022
- Final Protocol Submission: June 2023
- Study Completion: August 2024
- Final Report Submission: May 2025
L2217: ## PMR 4270-4
L2218: Conduct a post-marketing in vitro reaction phenotyping study with selective chemical inhibitors to determine the role of cytochrome P450 (CYP) enzymes (e.g., CYP3A4/5, CYP2B6, CYP2C19, CYP2C9, and CYP2D6) in vonoprazan metabolism at clinically relevant concentrations under the linear condition. This study should be designed and conducted in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry entitled “In Vitro Drug Interaction Studies — Cytochrome P450 Enzyme- and Transporter-Mediated Drug Interactions”. Depending on the results of this in vitro study, additional studies may be needed.
L2219: ## PMR Schedule Milestones

- Final Protocol Submission: September 2022
- Study Completion: December 2022
- Final Report Submission: March 2023

L2223: ## PMR 4270-5:
L2224: Conduct post-marketing in vitro DDI studies to evaluate the inhibition potential of vonoprazan metabolite M-I-G on CYP enzymes (except CYP3A) and transporters.

L2309: Figure 2 is a schematic diagram illustrating the Study Design of HP-301. The study timeline is divided into four visits (Visit 1, Visit 2, Visit 3, Visit 4) and includes a Screening Period, Treatment Period, and Follow-up Period.
Visit 1: Informed consent is obtained. This visit marks the start of the Screening Period (≤ 34 days).
Visit 2: Randomization occurs on Day -1. This visit marks the start of the Treatment Period (14 Days).
Visit 3: This visit marks the end of the Treatment Period and the start of the Follow-up Period (4 weeks).
Visit 4: This visit marks the end of the Follow-up Period and the start of the Eradication Assessment.
The treatment regimens administered during the Treatment Period (14 Days) are:
Vonoprazan Dual Therapy (Open Label)
Vonoprazan Triple Therapy (Double Blind)
Lansoprazole Triple Therapy (Double Blind)

L2314: Figure 8 displays eight line graphs showing Mean pH Time Profiles (0 to 24 Hours) in the Dose-Escalation Part, comparing Baseline (n=23) and Day 1 (n=9) for various doses of Voquezna Triple Pak (vonoprazan/clarithromycin/amoxicillin).
The graphs are arranged in two columns (Placebo vs. 1 mg to 120 mg) and four rows (5 mg, 10 mg, 20 mg, 40 mg, 80 mg, 120 mg).
Y-axis: pH (ranging from 0 to 9, or 0 to 5 for 1 mg). X-axis: Time (hr) (ranging from 0 to 24).
Legend (consistent across all plots): Baseline (n=23) and Day 1 (n=9).
Observations:
Placebo: Shows typical gastric pH fluctuations, with a baseline mean pH around 2.5-3.0. Day 1 shows a similar profile.
1 mg: Day 1 shows a significant increase in mean pH compared to Baseline, peaking around 6-7.
5 mg: Day 1 shows a significant increase in mean pH compared to Baseline, peaking around 6-7.
10 mg: Day 1 shows a significant increase in mean pH compared to Baseline, peaking around 6-7.
20 mg: Day 1 shows a significant increase in mean pH compared to Baseline, peaking around 7-8.
40 mg: Day 1 shows a significant increase in mean pH compared to Baseline, peaking around 7-8.
80 mg: Day 1 shows a significant increase in mean pH compared to Baseline, peaking around 7-8.
120 mg: Day 1 shows a significant increase in mean pH compared to Baseline, peaking around 7-8.
The overall trend indicates that increasing doses of the medication lead to a sustained elevation of mean gastric pH over the 24-hour period.
L2315: Figure 9 displays three graphs showing Mean pH Time Profiles (0 to 24 Hours) in the Food-Effect Part, comparing Placebo, 10 mg, and 40 mg doses of TAK-438 under Baseline, Fasted, and Fed conditions.
The Y-axis represents pH (ranging from 0 to 9) and the X-axis represents Time (hr) from 0 to 24.
Placebo (Top Left): Shows typical gastric pH fluctuations, with a baseline pH around 2-3. The Fasted condition shows a sharp peak around 5-6 pH around 4-6 hours. The Fed condition shows a similar peak but slightly lower pH values.
10 mg (Top Right): Shows a significant increase in post-dose stomach pH. The Fasted condition peaks around 7-8 pH around 4-6 hours. The Fed condition peaks around 7-8 pH around 10-12 hours. The Baseline pH remains around 2-3.
40 mg (Bottom Left): Shows the highest post-dose stomach pH. The Fasted condition peaks around 7-8 pH around 4-6 hours. The Fed condition peaks around 7-8 pH around 10-12 hours. The Baseline pH remains around 2-3.
In all graphs, the Fed condition generally shows a higher pH than the Fasted condition after the dose, indicating suppression of gastric acid secretion.

L2317: Figure 11 displays two graphs showing Mean Intragastric pH Versus Time Profiles. L2318: Figure 12 displays two graphs showing Mean Pre-dose Vonoprazan Plasma Concentrations Over Days 2 to 11. The left graph uses a Linear Scale (Y-axis: Concentration in ng/mL, 0 to 30). The right graph uses a Semi-Logarithmic Scale (Y-axis: Concentration in ng/mL, 1 to 100). Both graphs plot concentration versus Day (2 to 11). Data points are shown for two treatments: Midazolam+Vonoprazan (circles) and Vonoprazan alone (squares), with error bars indicating variability. The Vonoprazan alone group shows a rapid increase in concentration from Day 2 to Day 3, stabilizing around 15-18 ng/mL from Day 4 onwards. The Midazolam+Vonoprazan group shows a slower increase, stabilizing around 10-12 ng/mL from Day 4 onwards. The concentrations for the Midazolam+Vonoprazan group are consistently lower than the Vonoprazan alone group.

L2327: Figure 21 displays six plots showing Systemic Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) versus Time (h) for a single oral dose of Vonoprazan in healthy non-Japanese males. The plots are arranged in two rows of three columns, corresponding to doses of 10 mg, 15 mg, 20 mg, 30 mg, and 40 mg. Each plot compares observed data points (circles) with predicted concentration-time profiles (solid lines). The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 0.01 to 100 \mu\text{g/mL}, and the X-axis ranges from 0 to 40 hours. The concentration generally decreases over time, with higher doses showing higher peak concentrations and slightly longer half-lives.
L2328: Figure 22 displays six plots showing Systemic Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) versus Time (h) for once daily dosing of Vonoprazan in healthy non-Japanese subjects. The plots are arranged in two rows of three columns, corresponding to doses of 10 mg SD, 20 mg SD, and 30 mg SD (Single Dose, left column), and 10 mg QD, 20 mg QD, and 30 mg QD (Once Daily, right column). The left column plots show a single peak concentration followed by a decline. The right column plots show multiple peaks (oscillating concentrations) over 168 hours, indicating steady-state achievement. The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 0.1 to 100 \mu\text{g/mL} (SD plots) or 0 to 100 \mu\text{g/mL} (QD plots), and the X-axis ranges from 0 to 168 hours.
L2329: Figure 23 displays two graphs showing the Mean Values of Systemic concentration in plasma of Vonoprazan_V7_TDI with and without Interaction over Time using SV-Clarithromycin.
The left graph plots Systemic Concentration (ng/mL) on a linear scale (0 to 80) against Time - Substrate (h) (120 to 160). The right graph plots Systemic Concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.1 to 100.0) against Time - Substrate (h) (120 to 160).
In both graphs, the solid line (filled circles) represents vonoprazan concentration in the presence of clarithromycin, and the dashed line (open circles) represents vonoprazan concentration without interaction. The presence of clarithromycin significantly increases the maximum concentration (C_{max}) and the area under the curve (AUC) of vonoprazan.

The pathways contributing to systemic clearance are: CYP2C19 Liver, CYP2D6 Liver, CYP3A4 Liver, Additional HLM, and Renal.
